# Scribe ingest: copywriting knowledge in Google Drive and Notion that is NOT in the Drive export

- **Prepared:** 2026-11-25, read-only pass (nothing in Drive or Notion was edited, shared, moved or created).
- **Compared against:** `sops/drive-export/` (163 files) by Drive file ID, using the two inventory files. A doc counts as "new" if its ID is not in the export. Where a new doc's *text* was already inside an exported file (mainly export `001-X---2-Hour-Cold-Email-Campaign-SOP.md`, which embeds most of the Notion template library), that is flagged on the doc.
- **Searches run (Drive full text and titles):** cold email; copy / copywriting; spintax; subject line; email script; swipe; sequence; follow up; template; example; script; framework; offer; lead magnet; angle; thesis; CTA; first line; personalization; objection; winning copy; best performing; copy review; Eric Nowoslawski; Nick Abraham; Leadbird; Hormozi; Instantly; Smartlead; Twenty West; Lead Flo; Human Marketing; Marketing Cartel; MJJ; Global Media (Group); Dulcy. Notion: cold email copywriting; subject line spintax follow up sequence; email templates offer lead magnet; Eric Nowoslawski Nick Abraham Leadbird; client names; Hormozi offer CTA personalization first line objection framework. About 1,100 Drive hits (614 unique non-folder files), 48 already exported.
- **Read in full: 81 new sources** (13 Notion pages + 68 Drive docs/files, counting exact duplicates once each where read). All are written up below; sections group duplicates (e.g. Closers.io .docx/.pdf twins, Lead Academy pair, LuckyDiem pair). Lead-list sheets and CSVs were not opened as copy docs (they are data); see the skipped list.

## How to use this file (priority order for Scribe)

1. **Alex's own rules** (highest authority after `rules/RULES.md`): Agency Ignition docs D18-D31, D19 subject lines, D21 3 rules, D23 end-result scripts, D24 campaign types, D25 follow-ups, D28 pre-launch checklist, D33 2025 playbook, D35, D45, D47, D16, D12, D30, D7 TWM audit (Alex's critique of bad copy).
2. **Dulcy's latest client review drafts (2026):** D1 Lead FLO, D2 MJJ, D6/D42 Human, D8 TWM, D50 Shop Innovator. These show the current house style: offer-led, short, one question CTA, qualifiers on every claim, no breakup lines, no invented proof.
3. **Best-performer evidence:** D53 RMG "BEST REPLIES" (variants with reply counts), D55 campaign rundown (interest rates by angle), D60 LuckyDiem "This email got a quick open and response", D34 "Golden Scripts (Top Performers)".
4. **Swipe libraries:** N1 Notion Cold Email Playbook (largest template bank; ~65% already in export 001), N2, N3, D15, N12 whitepaper method.
5. **Third-party training:** D13/D14 (2026 YouTube framework), D37-D41 Closers.io, D54 Nick Abraham/Leadbird, D56 Lead Academy, D58 TrustScout, D17 Reddit pain mining, N4/N8/N9 prompt and template banks.
6. **Client baselines and anti-examples:** D5, D10 (MJJ client-written), D49 (Deal Flow 2023), D57, D48.

## Conflicts found between sources (ask Alex once, then log)

- Word count: "Less than 50 words" (N1, D33) vs "50-75 words / 4 sentences" (D21) vs "under 75 words" (D7) vs "30-75" (D13). Reading level: 3rd grade (N2) vs 4th (D21) vs 6th (D35).
- Follow-up spacing: Alex D25 "1–2 days apart", "2–3 steps"; D7 "4 days a bit long"; Closers.io 4-5 days (N3, D38, D59); N4 day 1/3/7/14; Human D42 4-5 days; MJJ D5 3-7 days.
- A/B threshold: one variable per 1,000+ leads (D28) vs 2k+ (N13) vs 250 per variant (N12, Isaiah) vs 100 sends (Closers.io D39).
- Open rates: "We also don’t track open rates" (D19, D54) vs open-rate goals 40-60% (N4) and Closers.io KPIs (D39).
- Guarantees: Alex's offer docs encourage scoped guarantees (D12, D30) vs 2026 client reviews forbid any lead/revenue guarantee not approved by the client (D1, D2).
- MJJ repair turnaround: "7-business-day" (client D5) vs "10 business days on agreed jobs" (Dulcy D2) vs "3–5 day" and "7-10" (client D10).
- Hard CTAs: N1 labels hard CTAs "Only for Database Reactivation"; D15 drops that label.

## N1. The Cold Email Playbook (Notion)

- **Link:** https://app.notion.com/p/1b617e50215681158aa0dd6874277ead
- **Path:** Dulcy Dashboard / The Cold Email Playbook (top-level hub page)
- **Last modified:** 2026-05-13 (page_last_edited_at 2026-05-13T17:21Z)
- **Author:** UNKNOWN (Notion page in the Dulcy workspace; no author field returned). Content style matches Alex's internal playbook; examples are mostly third-party swipes.
- **Not in export:** the Drive export has no copy of this page. Overlaps partly with export `088-COLD-EMAIL-SOURCE-OF-TRUTH.md` / `130-Dulcy-Cold-Email-Best-Practices.md`, but the template library, CTA ladder and P.S. bank below are not in the export.

**Summary.** Dulcy's master swipe file for cold email. It opens with six copywriting guidelines, defines four subject-line types, then holds ~80 full email templates tagged by use (#General, #Hyper, #DO = direct offer, #Interview, #SoftCTA, #RR = risk reversal, #Dulcy, #Ecom, #Localbiz, #Agencies, #HiringAgency, #Leadmag, #Podcast, #SEO, #RB2B, #Martech), a CTA ladder (Hard / Soft / Softest / "Top 16"), a P.S.-line bank and follow-up email templates. Links three child pages (N2, N3, N4 below) and "Client Offer Examples" (N5).

**Rules (exact quotes).**
- "Copywriting Guide Lines: No Fluff / Easy to say yes to / Offer so good they feel stupid to say no / Low CTA friction / Less than 50 words / High Value"
- Subject lines: "Now there are 4 types of subject lines: Desired Outcome SL, Pain SL, Reference SL, Curiously SL"
- Desired Outcome SL "is very good for end-result-based offers."
- Pain SL "is good if you are selling a pain-relieving outcome."
- Reference SL: "We use the subject line if we have a really good data point on our leads ... [we] don't use it as much, as it takes a long time to create these subject lines and we found better results with other forms of subject lines."
- Curiosity SL: "This is the most popular subject line we use as it promotes curiosity in our prospects."
- "A good ideology to keep in your mind when creating subject lines is making sure that the subject lines you write almost resemble a co-worker emailing another coworker."
- CTA ladder header: "Hard (Only for Database Reactivation)". Hard CTAs (e.g. "Worth a chat?") are labelled for database reactivation only; cold first touches use Soft / Softest.

**Frameworks.** 4 subject-line types; CTA friction ladder Hard -> Soft -> Softest; P.S. line types (Relevant/Personalized, Case Studies, RR/Guarantee, Opt Outs); "Ultimate Cold Email Sequence" 3 steps (case-study first line -> new proof + softer CTA -> "right person?" referral ask).

**Good/bad labels.** The page labels nothing as bad. All templates are presented as approved swipes (good, generic, no Dulcy client). Several contain third-party brand names and results (Larroude, Jack Rogers, Mailshake, Appcast, Sterling Caviar, ILoveKickBoxing, Liquid Death, goldpan.io, Russell Brunson etc.). These are **swipe-file examples from other companies, not Dulcy proof**. Never reuse their numbers as Dulcy or client results.

Copywriting Guide Lines:

- No Fluff
- Easy to say yes to
- Offer so good they feel stupid to say no
- Low CTA friction
- Less than 50 words
- High Value

Subject Lines

Now there are 4 types of subject lines:

1. Desired Outcome SL
2. Pain SL
3. Reference SL
4. Curiously SL

And you would use each of these for different situations.

Desired Outcome SL

- Helping your team with (outcome)
- Need more (outcome)?
- Looking for (outcome)?
- More (outcome)?

This subject line is very good for end-result-based offers. Offers like marketing, revenue increases, etc. I would use this in your subject line if your email copy is heavily end-result-based.

Pain SL

- Have trouble with (problem)?
- Not enough (problem)?
- Low reply rates?

This subject line is good if you are selling a pain-relieving outcome. Something like saving time or money by using your service. You could also use it to bring out a pain in the prospect that you can help fix with your service.

Reference SL

- Congrats on (accomplishment)
- Just saw (case study)
- Enjoyed (accomplishment)?

We use the subject line if we have a really good data point on our leads. We used to use it a lot more when we used AI to find out more data points on our prospects, but don't use it as much, as it takes a long time to create these subject lines and we found better results with other forms of subject lines.

Curiosity SL

- Quick question (first name)
- Real quick
- Have you seen this (first name)?
- Thoughts (first name)

This is the most popular subject line we use as it promotes curiosity in our prospects. It gives them an extra incentive to respond and see what they're missing out on.

---

Overall, the best subject lines are the ones that catch your prospects attention. These are quick and powerful words that give a strong incentive for your leads to actually look at your emails.

A good ideology to keep in your mind when creating subject lines is making sure that the subject lines you write almost resemble a co-worker emailing another coworker.

Example:

- Check this out
- We should try this
- Etc.

Make sure your subject lines are easy to read and get your prospects jumping on the phone to answer your emails.

> Subject Line Example

- Quick Question
- {FIRST_NAME}?
- thoughts {FIRST_NAME}?
- have you seen this {FIRST_NAME}?
- question {FIRST_NAME}
- can I get your opinion {FIRST_NAME}?
- opinion {FIRST_NAME}
- [your service] question
- question about [your service]
- [your service]?
- our [your service]
- {RESTAURANT}’s food - yes or no?
- {RESTAURANT}?
- re:[your service]
- [service]

Full Cold Email Templates

### General

> Ultimate Cold Email Sequence

- Step 1

## N2. Scripts/Lead Magnets that have generated $100M+ over the last 2 years… (Notion)

- **Link:** https://app.notion.com/p/23a17e50215680fdb969f2c0cba98bd1
- **Path:** Dulcy Dashboard / The Cold Email Playbook
- **Last modified:** 2026-07-30
- **Author:** UNKNOWN (Notion; the "$100M+" claim is the page title, origin of the scripts is not stated, treat as third-party swipe).

**Summary.** Lead-magnet ladder (Level 1-3 by perceived value), lead-magnet ideas per agency niche, an "Offer Introduction" bank of guarantee-style offer statements by industry (identical to N5 "Client Offer Examples"), script-writing rules, two base scripts, the "Ultimate Cold Email Sequence" (same as N1) and a personalization source list.

**Rules (exact quotes).**
- "A good lead magnet solves a problem for lead who then introduces a new problem which is solved by your offer."
- "Make an offer so good, that people feel stupid for saying now" [sic, "no"]
- "Ranking- More perceived value more likely the sale"
- "You have black and white text. You have 1 email to convince someone to book a meeting with you depending on your offer and niche."
- "Leverage a part of your service that already works to generate a lead magnet. A lead magnet provides a complete solution to a very narrow problem."
- "Always confirm each script is below 3rd grade reading level. Run this into Chatgpt and say make this script as short and simple as possible without losing its core value."
- "If you contact someone multiple times then you show that you are serious. Curiosity increases because they fear they're missing out."
- "5-6 touchpoints on linkedin, sms, call, email."
- Tool named: Hemingway Editor (https://hemingwayapp.com/).

**Framework: lead-magnet levels (verbatim).**
- Level 1- White paper, Webinar, Mini Course, E-Book, Templates, Survey
- Level 2- Samples, Free Trials, Free Tools, Free Evaluation
- Level 3- Leads, Calls Booked, Ad creatives, Listing Optimization, Service Sample, Free Course, Free Software, Landing page for free, if it converts 5% then you pay me

**Lead magnets by agency type (verbatim).**
- PPC: "5 personalization ad creatives based on your top competitors" (bold in source); Google Ads audit + one rewritten campaign structure; Free performance projection using your current ROAS + market benchmarks (marketing titles); 3 retargeting ad concepts (image + copy); 3 scroll-stopping ad hooks; 1 free retargeting campaign build; Free Ad Creatives + Copy; We will build you a new ad campaign if it outperforms your current one you pay us $2500; Free Database reactivation - retargeting ads to existing leads, or previous calls who havent closed. (direct roi)
- SEO / SEO AI: 10 intent-clustered keywords for one product/service; 5 backlinks you're missing; AI GPT audit
- Web Dev: 1-page redesign; Free Wireframe; Free Landing Page; Broken feature repair; "Free website mockup" (bold); Free speed optimization (3 bottlenecks removed)
- Lead Generation: 1,000 Free Leads; Free email script; Book a call for them; if we book you 10-20 prospects; Free Database reactivation - retargeting email campaigns to existing leads, or previous calls who havent closed. (direct roi)
- Content/Video: Free video; 2 short-form videos edited from your existing raw footage; Podcast -> content conversion of one episode; Free UGC Content; We'll turn your last CONTENT video into a full campaign; Free video hook testing: 3 hooks we'll script + edit for you
- AI: Free AI Build; Free Demo of AI; 1 free AI automation flow (lead routing, email replies, etc.); Free chatbot trained on your site; Free automation setup
- Amazon Marketing: Free Amazon listing rewrite; 1 PPC campaign build; Free competitor keyword tear-down; Free Listing Optimization; We will build you a new ad campaign if it outperforms your current one you pay us $2500; We will increase your sales by X and you only pay if we do; Free ASIN listing rewrite with backend keyword update
- Software Development: Free MVP scope doc
- Influencer Marketing: List of influencers
- Print & Direct Mail: 1 free mailer design; Free printed sample pack; 1 free postcard mockup
- Branding, Creative, PR: Press pitch template

**Offer Introduction bank.** Identical text to N5 "Client Offer Examples" (see N5 for the full verbatim list). Generic third-party offer statements; numbers are illustrative, not Dulcy proof.

**Script templates (verbatim).**

> Base 1
> Hi Name,
> I noticed you on X and I'm reaching out because of Y.
> We USP that solves Z in Timeframe without No good rotten thing.
> Offer something for free.

> Base 2
> Hi FNAME - if we could {OFFER}, would you be interested?
> Signature
> P.S. with Case study

> Ultimate Cold Email Sequence: same 3 steps as in N1 (Step 1 AI first line + competitor case study + CTA; Step 2 variation + other proof + softer CTA; Step 3 "Since you are the {TITLE} at {COMPANY}, thought you would be the right person to reach out to regarding {your offer}. Would you be the right person for this?").

**Personalization sources (verbatim list).** Find Website Visitors; Employee 1 or 2; Find Competitors; Find Ads - Generate Ideas- Write an icebreaker; Personalized based on website/linkedin data; Case Study/ Customers; Suggest Leads; Potential Clients; Recently left jobs; Funding; Restaurants; Check for SEO; Product call out.

**Good/bad labels:** none; all presented as good generic swipes.


## N3. Cold Email Chris Scripts from Closers.io (Notion)

- **Link:** https://app.notion.com/p/2f417e50215680bf8c17c237a66d18cd
- **Path:** Dulcy Dashboard / The Cold Email Playbook
- **Last modified:** 2026-01-26
- **Author:** notes taken from "Chris" at Closers.io training (third-party source); note-taker UNKNOWN. Same material appears in Drive as `Initial Email Creatives` and `Follow Up Creatives For Cold Email` (D-docs below, Closers.io course export).

**Summary.** Closers.io's cold email creatives: a test-every-angle-in-email-1 approach, Loom pitch, "Trojan Horse" Loom, free lead magnet, guarantee/risk reversal, "dual layered" offer, and a 3-email follow-up structure where email 2 is email 1 with personalization removed ("billboard drive by").

**Rules (exact quotes).**
- "Tests all angles in email 1 to see which one does best and then run with it for the next 10-20-30 thousand."
- "EMAIL 2 - wait 4 days (take inital email, get rid of personalization and paste it - bill board drive by)"
- "Email 3 - wait 3 days"
- Opener device: "I'll skip the fluff and get right to it:"

**Frameworks.** Angle types: Loom Video Pitch; Trojan Horse (I noticed a few things hurting X, recorded a video); Free Lead Magnet (built the asset already); Guarantee / risk reversal; Dual layered (two stacked deliverables). Sequence: 1 (A/B/C/D angle test) -> 2 (+4 days, depersonalized repeat, CTA = watch video) -> 3 (+3 days, short video offer).

**Examples (verbatim; all third-party Closers.io examples, label: good / generic, not Dulcy proof).**

The Initial Email Creative
SL: Quick question , Tiktok Question for NAME , 1 million TIkTok views for COMPANY

> Saw you're in charge of COMPANY social media.
> I'll skip the fluff and get right to it:

Loom Video Pitch
> We can drive millions of eyeballs for your company across social media (even if TikTok gets banned) with our Organic Viral Storytelling - consistently.
> Your company will reach Gen Z with a new format that few businesses know about (we only have about 15 clients).
> Interested in a quick Loom explainer going over a content strategy I mapped out for you?

Bonus Loom pitch - Trojan Horse

> I put together a quick video breaking down the causes and how to fix them, mind if I share the video?
> —
> I was checking out COMPANY welcome email and I noticed a few things that are your conversions with new customers
> —

> I was checking out COMPANY Google profile and I noticed you have a few bad reviews that is hurting your reputation.

> If not, I can build you a new one in 2 weeks.

Guarantee / risk reversal

> Open to learning more?
> —
> We can get you 1 million views on TikTok in the next 90 days - guaranteed or else we'll send you a refund.

Dual layered

Another example

## N4. ChatGPT/Claude Prompts (Notion)

- **Link:** https://app.notion.com/p/1cd17e5021568094a1bdcaacf5ca33c7
- **Path:** Dulcy Dashboard / ChatGPT/Claude Prompts (child page: Custom GPTS)
- **Last modified:** 2025-08-24
- **Author:** UNKNOWN (Dulcy workspace; prompt library, mostly third-party prompts).
- **Read:** the whole page (~132k characters after cleanup). About 21% of long lines are already in the export. Lines ~387-1671 of the page are a full copy of N1 "The Cold Email Playbook" (subject lines, templates, CTAs, P.S., follow-ups) embedded as knowledge inside the responder prompt; not repeated here.

**Copy-relevant contents.**
1. **Prompt Creator** (generic prompt-iteration prompt).
2. **Customer Profile Generator** (ICP interview -> JSON customer avatar). Useful for Scribe's research step.
3. **$20K growth consultant** (competitive landscape prompt; not copy).
4. **Spintax Email Scripts** — identical to the D18 GPT spintax prompt (see D18 for the verbatim text).
5. **The Perfect Cold Email Responder** — a reply-handling GPT (Sean's area). Its knowledge block, "The Sell-by-Email Playbook", contains copy rules (quoted below), then appends the whole N1 playbook ("Here is everything I’ve written about cold email before to help you.").
6. **The Perfect LinkedIn DM Responder** (LinkedIn; not email; skipped beyond a skim).
7. Generic business prompts (growth plan, domain generator, market research, legal drafting, negotiation, "Write a professional email", brainstorming, blog, business plan, translation). Not cold email copy.

**Prompt (verbatim) — Prompt Creator.**
> I want you to become my Prompt Creator. Your goal is to help me craft the best possible prompt for my needs. The prompt will be used by you, ChatGPT. You will follow the following process:
> 1. Your first response will be to ask me what the prompt should be about.
> I will provide my answer, but we will need to improve it through continual iterations by going through the next steps.
> 2. Based on my input, you will generate 3 sections. a) Revised prompt (provide your rewritten prompt. it should be clear, concise, and easily understood by you), b) Suggestions (provide suggestions on what details to include in the prompt to improve it), and c) Questions (ask any relevant questions pertaining to what additional information is needed from me to improve the prompt).
> 3. We will continue this iterative process with me providing additional information to you and you updating the prompt in the Revised prompt section until it's complete.

**Prompt (verbatim) — Customer Profile Generator.**
> ROLE: Expert Marketing Consultant & Customer Profile Specialist
> EXPERIENCE: 15+ years helping businesses identify ideal customers
> TASK: Interview user, make intelligent conclusions, create detailed customer avatar in JSON format
> SYSTEM INSTRUCTIONS:
> 1. Introduce as marketing consultant 2. Ask one question at a time from question list 3. Wait for responses before proceeding 4. Analyze each response and make expert conclusions 5. Store responses and conclusions in JSON structure 6. Maintain professional but friendly tone 7. Guide user if answers need more detail 8. Focus on extracting actionable insights 9. Present final JSON profile after all questions 10. Use provided JSON structure
> JSON STRUCTURE: {"customer_avatar": {"basic_demographics": {"age_range": "", "gender": "", "location": "", "income_level": "", "education": "", "occupation": "", "expert_demographic_insights": ""}, "psychographics": {"values": [], "interests": [], "lifestyle": "", "personality_traits": [], "goals": [], "pain_points": [], "expert_psychographic_insights": ""}, "buying_behavior": {"purchase_triggers": [], "decision_factors": [], "price_sensitivity": "", "preferred_channels": [], "research_habits": "", "expert_behavior_insights": ""}, "media_consumption": {"preferred_platforms": [], "content_types": [], "influencers_followed": [], "daily_online_hours": "", "expert_media_insights": ""}, "brand_relationship": {"brand_expectations": [], "communication_preferences": [], "loyalty_factors": [], "objections": [], "expert_brand_insights": ""}, "marketing_recommendations": {"suggested_channels": [], "content_strategy": "", "messaging_approach": "", "unique_selling_propositions": [], "optimization_opportunities": []}}}
> INTERVIEW QUESTIONS (Ask one at a time):
> 1. "What is the age range and gender of your ideal customer?" 2. "Where are they typically located and what's their approximate income level?" 3. "What is their educational background and current occupation?" 4. "What are their top 3 values and main interests?" 5. "Describe their lifestyle and key personality traits" 6. "What are their main goals and biggest pain points?" 7. "What triggers them to make a purchase and what factors influence their buying decisions?" 8. "How price-sensitive are they and where do they prefer to shop?" 9. "Which social media platforms do they use most and what type of content do they consume?" 10. "What do they expect from brands and how do they prefer to be communicated with?"
> AFTER EACH RESPONSE: - Analyze information provided - Make expert marketing conclusions - Add insights to relevant "expert_insights" fields - Connect patterns between different answers - Identify marketing opportunities
> AFTER ALL RESPONSES: 1. Fill "marketing_recommendations" section based on collected data 2. Present complete customer avatar profile in JSON format 3. Ask: "I've created your customer avatar profile with my expert insights. Would you like to review any part of the profile or make adjustments?"
> INTRODUCTION SCRIPT: "Hello, I'm a senior marketing consultant with over 15 years of experience helping businesses deeply understand their ideal customers. I specialize in turning customer insights into actionable marketing strategies that drive real results. Today, I'll help you create a detailed profile of your ideal customer through a series of targeted questions.

**Prompt header (verbatim) — The Perfect Cold Email Responder, Prompt 1.**
> Prompt 1: The Sell-by-Email Playbook Knowledge
> You are about to receive essential content containing teachings, frameworks, and rules for selling by cold email. It is crucial that you strictly adhere to this content in all future interactions.
> Instructions:
> • Strict Adherence: Base all your future recommendations, strategies, and outputs solely on the provided content. Do not introduce any external information or deviate from the material.
> • Analyze Before Responding: Before generating any response, carefully analyze the provided content to ensure your reply is relevant and accurate based on the specified teachings, frameworks, and rules.
> • Consistency: Do not offer suggestions or practices not contained within the content. Avoid assumptions or using external knowledge beyond this material.
> • Clarity and Relevance: Ensure all your responses are clear, concise, and directly applicable to tasks related to selling by cold email, as outlined in the content.

**"Sell-by-Email Playbook" copy rules (exact quotes; generic, author UNKNOWN, reads AI-written).**
- "What actually works is genuinely helping people—reaching out with relevance, empathy, and clear value."
- "Not a pitch. Not a wall of text. Just a clear, conversational message that starts with value and ends with a low-friction ask."
- "Don’t sell. Serve."
- "The Cold Email Flow Framework: 1. Pattern Interrupt – Subject line or first sentence must break the norm and earn attention. 2. Personalized Relevance – Reference something that shows this isn’t a copy-paste blast. 3. Clear Value Proposition – What’s in it for them? Why should they keep reading? 4. Soft, Low-Friction CTA – Make it stupid-easy to respond or say yes. No hard commitments."
- "Preloaded Value: Lead with something useful. A stat, a resource, a short idea that solves a piece of their problem." Examples: “Noticed your team’s hiring—mind if I send over a few ways our clients are automating outbound so reps focus on closing?” / “Just helped a team like yours increase booking rate by 42%. Can share the framework if helpful.”
- Micro-commitment CTAs: “Want me to send more details?” / “Would this be helpful to see?” / “Open to a quick breakdown?”
- Personalization rules: "Be Human. Drop the robotic tone. Avoid “Hi there” or “Hope this finds you well.”" / "Reference Their World ... “Noticed you’re expanding into healthcare—how’s that rollout going?”" / "Use Plain Language" BAD: “We’re a cutting-edge, synergy-driven SaaS platform that leverages AI to optimize…” GOOD: “We help SaaS teams get 30% more demos without hiring another SDR.”
- Metrics: "Open Rate (goal: 40–60%) / Reply Rate (goal: 8–15%) / Positive Reply Rate (goal: 3–6%) / Meeting Booked Rate (goal: 1–3%)"
- "80% of responses come from follow-ups." Example follow-up: "Subject: Just checking / Hey {FirstName}, / Not sure if my last note landed—happy to send over a few examples if it’s helpful. / Want me to?"
- Follow-up timing: "Day 1 (initial) / Day 3 / Day 7 / Day 14 / Then weekly for 3–5 touches total"
- Objection replies: “Totally makes sense. Just out of curiosity—are you looking to improve [goal] later this year?” / “Appreciate the honesty—mind if I check back in [X months]?”

**Conflicts with Alex's own rules (flag).** Open-rate goals conflict with D19 ("We also don’t track open rates"). Day 1/3/7/14 + weekly timing conflicts with D25 (2–3 steps, 1–2 days apart). "Just checking" subject conflicts with D7/D25 bans on lazy bumps. Treat this block as generic AI-written content, lower authority than Alex's Agency Ignition docs.

**Summary.** A bank of risk-reversal offer statements by niche, in the pattern "We'll [specific result] in [timeframe], or [penalty to us / you don't pay]". Useful as an offer-line pattern library. **All numbers are illustrative generic examples. None are Dulcy or client proof. Never put these numbers in client copy.**

**Rule (exact quote).** "Here are a few offer statements we've run across several different niches."

**Framework.** Result + timeframe + risk reversal (refund, "you don't pay", or a personal penalty such as a gift card / dinner / "invoice me your hourly rate").

## N6. High-Converting Cold Email Copy (Notion, client-portal training page)

- **Link (read):** https://app.notion.com/p/21f17e5021568106bd2ece29d209c2f3 ([Client] Template version, last edited 2025-06-27)
- **Path:** Dulcy Dashboard / Client Portals / Onboarding Hub / [Client] Template / PHASE 1 → Building Your Sales Machine / OFFER - PREPARING FOR OUTBOUND
- **Author:** UNKNOWN (Dulcy client-onboarding portal content; client-facing training). Copies of this page exist in each older client portal (Luckydiem, Visimpact, Adverio, Social Slogan, Amazon Consultancy, A-Wing Visuals, ForeverCard, Snowball Print Marketing, and an older [Client] Template version 2025-03-20). Search snippets show the same headings; treated as duplicates and not fetched one by one.

**Summary.** Client-facing explainer: why cold emails get ignored, how to position the offer, three frameworks (Authority & Social Proof, Problem-Agitate-Solution, "Right Person?"), personalization vs relevance, and the best CTAs. Links a child page "High-Converting Direct Scripts".

**Rules (exact quotes).**
- "A cold email isn’t just an introduction—it’s a strategic tool to spark interest and start a real conversation."
- Why emails get ignored: "They sound like every other outreach email. / They focus on what they do instead of what the prospect cares about. / There’s no compelling reason to respond—no urgency, no curiosity, no clear benefit."
- "Cold email isn’t about explaining everything upfront—it’s about making the reader feel like, “This is worth replying to.”"
- "Your offer alone won’t get replies—it has to be positioned in a way that makes the reader care instantly."
- Key elements: "Immediate relevance—Why should they care? / Pain-driven hook—Call out the problem they already know they have. / Easy-to-understand offer—Clear, concise, no jargon. / Risk reversal or intrigue—Remove hesitation by making it low-risk to engage. / Low-effort CTA—Make it simple for them to say “yes” to the next step."
- "Personalization is a tool, not a strategy." / "Relevance matters more than personalization." / "Personalization is only effective if it makes the email feel relevant to them."
- "Your call-to-action should feel effortless."
- "Your goal isn’t to “sell” in the email—it’s to start a conversation."

**Examples (verbatim; labels from source).**
- BAD: “We offer LinkedIn growth services to help you build your brand.” BETTER: “I noticed {Company} isn’t leveraging LinkedIn for inbound leads. We helped {Similar Company} add $250K in pipeline last quarter just by fixing that. Want me to send over the breakdown?” (Why: "It’s specific / It’s pain-driven / It creates curiosity / It makes replying easy")
- The Authority & Social Proof Approach (best for positioning as an expert):
> Hey {FirstName},
> We recently helped {Company} increase {key metric} by {percentage} in {timeframe}, and I think we can do the same for {CompanyName}.
> Want me to send over the exact strategy we used?
- The Problem-Agitate-Solution Approach (best for a pain they ignore):
> Hey {FirstName},
> How many trial users drop off before they even experience your product? Most SaaS companies think they need more traffic, but their onboarding process is killing conversions.
> Want me to show you how to fix it?
- The “Right Person?” Approach (low-pressure):
> Hey {FirstName},
> We’ve been helping {niche} companies generate {result} using {method}. Just helped {case study} add {X amount} to their pipeline in {timeframe}. We could do the same for {COMPANY}
> Would you be the right person to speak to?
- BAD personalization: “Saw your recent post on LinkedIn, great insights! We help SaaS companies scale lead generation…” BETTER: “Saw {Company} just launched {feature}. One of the biggest mistakes we see is X—want me to show you how to avoid that?”
- BAD CTA: “Would you be open to a 30-minute call to learn more?” BETTER: “Want me to send over the exact strategy we used?” Best variations: “Want me to send over a quick breakdown?” / “Would it be helpful if I shared a few examples?” / “Mind if I send over a short video explaining this?”

## N9. RB2B Email Templates — "Ultimate Email Template Guide" (Notion)

- **Link (read):** https://app.notion.com/p/1b717e50215681beaee2da1758b9c74a (last edited 2025-03-15; path Dulcy Dashboard / Client-Facing SOPs / High-Converting Direct Scripts / Internal SOPs). Seven more copies sit inside client portals ([Client] Template x2, Adverio, Amazon Consultancy, A-Wing Visuals, Luckydiem, Visimpact, Social Slogan); same title and opening line in search results; treated as duplicates.
- **Author:** third-party (RB2B's template guide, copied into Notion). Not Dulcy-written.

**Summary.** 14 generic outreach templates (cold intro, follow-up, pain + solution + proof, feedback ask, "just checking in", mutual connection, event follow-up, competitor switch, free value, personalized video, referral request, podcast listener, content compliment, direct and bold).

**Rule (exact quote).** "Remember, email templates are best used as a source of inspiration. Always personalize your emails and tailor them to your specific audience for the best results!"

**Label vs Dulcy rules: mostly BAD as written.** Most end with a call ask ("Would you be open to a quick chat sometime next week?"), which Dulcy rules call too high for a cold first touch (D7, D23, N6). "Hope you're doing well!" is banned in N4 ("Avoid “Hi there” or “Hope this finds you well.”"). "Just wanted to follow up on my previous email" is the lazy bump banned in D7/D25. Links in first touch conflict with D21 ("no links"). Usable ideas: Competitor Switch, Free Value Offer, Personalized Video (with a soft CTA swapped in).

**Templates (verbatim).**
- The Cold Intro. Subject: [Their Company Name] - Quick Question. "Hi [Name], I'm [Your Name] with [Your Company]. We help [target persona] like you [briefly describe the key benefit of your product/service]. Noticed [something specific about their company or work, e.g., recent news, blog post, shared connection]. Thought it was interesting! Would you be open to a quick chat sometime next week? Best, [Your Name]"
- The Follow Up. Subject: Checking In: Feedback on [Product Idea] for [Target Persona]. "Hey [Name], Just wanted to follow up on my previous email. Still hoping to get your feedback on a new product idea for [target persona]. Even 10 minutes of your time would be incredibly valuable. Let me know if you're available for a quick chat this week! Best, [Your Name]"
- The "Pain Point + Solution + Social Proof" Play. Subject: Struggling with [Specific Pain Point]?. "Hey [Name], Are you struggling with [specific pain point]? I ask because [your solution] can help. [Customer testimonial or data point showing how your solution helps] Would you be open to a quick call to see how it could work for you? Best, [Your Name]"
- The Feedback Play. Subject: Quick Chat? New Product Idea for [Target Persona]. "Hey [Name], Love your work in [Industry/Area of Expertise]! Building something new to help [target persona] with [specific pain point], and would love your quick feedback. Mind hopping on a 15-min call sometime this week? Happy to send you a [incentive, e.g., gift card, free trial] as a thank you. Cheers, [Your Name]"
- The "Just Checking In" Email. Subject: Catching Up & [Relevant Industry Topic]. "Hey [Name], Noticed your recent post/article/activity about [relevant industry topic]. Thought it was insightful! On a separate note, I've been working on something that might be relevant to your work at [their company]. Would you be open to a quick chat sometime next week? Best, [Your Name]"
- The "Mutual Connection" Email. Subject: Connecting through [Mutual Connection's Name]. "Hi [Name], I'm reaching out at the suggestion of our mutual connection, [Mutual Connection's Name]. They thought we should connect, as we're both working in the [Industry/Area of Expertise] space. I'm particularly interested in [mention something specific about their work or company]. Would you be open to a quick introductory call sometime next week? Best, [Your Name]" (Only if the connection is real; D2 bans "false prior relationship".)
- The "Industry Event Follow-Up" Email. Subject: Great to Connect at [Event Name]!. "Hi [Name], It was great meeting you at [Event Name] the other day! I particularly enjoyed our conversation about [topic discussed]. As promised, I'm following up with [resource mentioned, e.g., link to article, information about your product]. Would you be open to a quick call sometime next week to discuss this further? Best, [Your Name]"
- The "Competitor Switch" Email. Subject: Making the Switch from [Competitor Name]?. "Hi [Name], I noticed you're currently using [Competitor Name]. We've been helping many businesses like yours make the switch to [Your Product] because [mention key advantages or differentiators]. Would you be open to a quick call to see if [Your Product] might be a better fit for your needs? Best, [Your Name]"
- The "Free Value Offer" Email. Subject: Free [Resource] to Help You [Benefit]. "Hey [Name], I thought you might find this helpful: [Link to free resource, e.g., guide, template, webinar recording] It's designed to help you [specific benefit, e.g., improve your lead generation, streamline your workflow, increase your productivity]. No strings attached, just hoping it provides some value! Cheers, [Your Name]"
- The "Personalized Video" Email. Subject: A Quick Video for You, [Name]. "Hi [Name], I put together a quick video to introduce myself and share how [Your Product] can help [Their Company] with [Specific pain point]. [Link to personalized video] Would love to hear your thoughts! Best regards, [Your Name]"
- The "Referral Request" Email. Subject: Seeking Introductions to [Target Persona]. "Hi [Name], Hope you're doing well! I'm currently building a new product to help [target persona] with [specific pain point]. I'm looking to connect with a few folks in that role to gather feedback and insights. Would you be willing to introduce me to anyone you know who might be a good fit? Thanks in advance for your help! Best, [Your Name]"
- The "Podcast Listener" Email. Subject: Loved Your Insights on [Podcast Name]!. "Hey [Name], Just wanted to say how much I enjoyed listening to you on the [Podcast Name] podcast! Your insights on [specific topic] were spot on. On a separate note, I'm working on something that might be relevant to your work at [their company] - [briefly describe your product/service]. Would you be open to a quick chat sometime next week? Cheers, [Your Name]"
- The "Content Compliment" Email. Subject: Great Article on [Topic]!. "Hi [Name], I recently came across your article on [topic] and found it incredibly insightful. Especially loved the part about [mention something specific]. I'm also working in the [industry/area of expertise] space, and I'm currently developing [briefly describe your product/service]. Would you be open to connecting sometime and sharing your thoughts on what I'm building? Best, [Your Name]"
- The "Direct and Bold" Email. Subject: [Their Company Name] + [Your Company Name] = 🚀. "Hey [Name], I believe [Your Product] can significantly help [Their Company] [achieve specific outcome, e.g., increase sales, improve efficiency, reduce costs]. I've put together a quick one-pager that outlines the key benefits: [Link to one-pager] Let's schedule a brief call to discuss how we can help you achieve your goals. Best regards, [Your Name]"

## N11. Email Sequence Setup (Notion, Deal Flux client portal) — warm-list email, not cold

- **Link:** https://app.notion.com/p/e4d865fa61aa4c4d8b7b356a65abd08f (last edited 2025-05-08; path Deal Flux / Clients / NAME / PHASE 3)

> Chances are, it’s been awhile since you’ve seen or interacted with any of my emails.
> Clearly, I’ve been quiet. I’ve been so busy working on some exciting new projects for you, that I haven’t done a very good job at helping you out in other ways.

> What we intend to do moving forward is to find ways to [RESULT AVATAR WANTS].
> Maybe we’ll [ACTION STEP].

## N12. Whitepaper Cold Email Approach (Notion)

- **Link:** https://app.notion.com/p/21b17e5021568091a8b8e5066ea82cbe
- **Path:** Dulcy Dashboard / The Cold Email Playbook
- **Last modified:** 2025-07-08
- **Author:** Alexander Woung notes from a call (page opens with a Fathom link labelled "Alexander Woung"); the method is taught by **Isaiah** (Isaiah Lebron, named in the forwarded-email template; outside expert, role UNKNOWN). The "Raw call summary" is an AI summary of that call.
- **Overlap:** export `131-Dulcy-WhitepaperEducation-Approach.md` has 2026 whitepaper headlines and interview questions; it does **not** have the rules, the forwarded-email template, the headline prompt or the testing process below.

**Rules (exact quotes).**
- "Use the script word for word"
- "dont say report say white paper"
- "always use current year in whitepaper"
- "3 step sequence with more info indepth"
- "If company isnt available to mention just dont include it"
- "whitepaper headline needs to stand out and not seem a part of the script"
- Summary: "Leverages social proof through vague references (e.g., “recently spoke to teams at…”), but must avoid using names unless true."
- "Subject Line & First Sentence: Whitepaper headline (capitalized like a report title)."
- "Isaiah prefers “Would you be open…” for softer tone and higher conversions."
- "Each script/variant must capital-case the whitepaper headline (e.g., “The Hidden Threat of AI in Retail Marketing”)."
- "For replies: Always route them straight to Calendly, not nurture-heavy unless totally cold or confused."
- Follow-ups: "2–3 steps. Share insights or findings from calls (“3 things [name] mentioned on how they’re winning”). Pull transcript lines or real takeaways if possible." / "If client said this is what has worked with you from the case study it can be in the follow ups as bullet points"
- "Whitepaper CTA phrasing and headline are critical — one bad headline can kill performance." / "Switching out the phrase “analyze report” to “whitepaper” is a tiny tweak that made a big difference."
- Client explanation: "“The way we got you on a call is the exact same way we’ll get your clients on a call.”"

**Framework: testing process (verbatim).** "Each campaign = 2,500 leads, with 10 variants of the same script but different whitepaper headlines. Each variant = 250 leads. KPI = 2% positive reply rate per variant (i.e. 5 replies). If a variant hits KPI, it moves to the next test round where Isaiah tweaks a word (e.g., “Next-Gen” → “Modern”) to improve. Continue testing until diminishing returns (e.g., can’t get above 3%)."

**Framework: keynote follow-on.** "A follow-up strategy used after whitepaper campaigns have hit TAM saturation. Framed as a personalized executive keynote". Script: "First Line: “We’re arranging an executive orientation on how [trend] is impacting [niche].” Second Line: “This session covers [quick benefit bullets or insights preview].” CTA: “Mind if I share more info?”"

**Bad result recorded (verbatim).** "A campaign targeting 7–8 figure Amazon brands using the whitepaper approach saw 8,000 contacts → 7 replies → 0 bookings." (Client UNKNOWN; Dulcy-run campaign result.)

**Example emails (verbatim).**
- Interview White Paper = N1 template (good; "Use the script word for word").
- NEW — Interview CEO Forwarded Email (good):
> Subject Line: Interview <> {FIRST_NAME}
> Hi {FIRST_NAME}, my colleague Isaiah came across your company on LinkedIn and asked me to reach out.
> We’ve been interviewing a few CEOs and business leaders in the recruiting & staffing space as we’re creating a study on How Lead Volume & Lead Quality Became The Biggest Bottleneck For Recruiting and Staffing Companies in 2025.
> We’ve already spoken to 4 leaders and got interesting insights.
> Totally fine to pass if it’s not a fit, but would you be open to a quick conversation to cover the insights we’ve gathered and your perspective?
> PS. We can keep the interview anonymous if you prefer.
> — - - - - - -Forwarded Messaged - - - - - -
> From: Isaiah Lebron
> Sent: Wednesday, March 27th, 2024 8:57:10 AM
> To: Luc Landenburger
> Suject: Reach out to {FIRST_NAME} - Interview
> Hey Luc, I was searching for marketing CEOs, and leaders that came across {FIRST_NAME}. Looks like a great person for our whitepaper.
> Worth you reaching out.
> Isaiah Lebron
(Note: the forwarded block must reflect a real colleague and real search; per D2 do not fake a prior relationship. "We’ve already spoken to 4 leaders" must be true.)
- "Interview Our Founder soft CTA - keynote (more direct offer focused should be)" = N1 template, labelled here as the keynote variant that "should be" more direct-offer focused.
- Other interview templates = N1.
- Follow-up 1 (verbatim draft): "Hey, we want to share this insight that John shared with us about how he's taking vast pieces of market share. Here's the three bulletins that he said, well, that he mentioned to us on how he's taking a lot of clients in his niche. - d - d - d (use transcript to get top bullet points from the call)"
- Reply template "Interview: Where will the whitepaper be posted / why" (verbatim, reply stage): "Thanks for the reply. Of course, essentially we're looking to get an updated idea of where the market is currently in terms of the problems & challenges that marketing agensies are facing, the goals they have and what they're doing on a day to day basis. As well as collecting this info for ourselves, Our goal is not only to share the insights that we've received from speaking other experts like yourself on the call with you, but we're also compiling these insights into a whitepaper to benefit the wider industry which should be completed around mid January; which we'll be sharing with who's contributed giving you early access, and then also going onto publish the whitepaper on our own social profiles at a later date. In terms of benefits on your side - youll receive insights to top level marketing agencies are operating and challenges theyre facing on the call, as well as early access to whitepaper which covers these topics at length. I'm free on [DAY] or [DAY] at [TIME] or (TIME] on (TIMEZONE] il you wish to chat. Alternatively here's my booking link it you wish to find another time: Speak soon, Name" ("Interview: Is this a sales Pitch?" reply is an empty toggle.)
- Spintax soft CTAs: “Would you be open to a quick chat?” / “Open to a 15-minute conversation to share findings?” / “Mind if I loop you into this?”

**Prompt (verbatim) — whitepaper headline ChatGPT prompt.**
> Step 1: What are the most common and impactful trends in the INDUSTRY niche in 2025-2026 (use research)
> Step 2: This is the information our client has provided us about their service, icp information, and client emotional journey. Take in this information and prepare for step 3.
> What EXACTLY do you sell to your Target Market?:
> What are some PROBLEMS your ideal clients have that you can solve for them?:
> Before working with you, what were your clients doing to solve the problem your offer now solves?
> How did that make them feel emotionally?
> What did they find most appealing or different about your approach?
> Step 3:
> Turn these trends and leverage the information about what the client does into white paper headlines that mimic these examples:
> Top 5 Emerging Businesses Development Trends Going Into 2025
> Top 5 Ways Fashion Brands Are Using AI to Drive Visibility and Brand Awareness in 2025.
> How Apparel and Fashion Brands are Leveraging AI to Drive Greater Brand Awareness, Engagement, and Revenue in 2025.
> The most disturbing things about XYZ
> Top 7 Branding Trends Driving Amazon E-Commerce Growth in 2025
> How Lead Volume & Lead Quality Became The Biggest Bottleneck For Recruiting and Staffing Companies in 2024
> How AI Is Changing The Learning Design Process And How You Can Use It To Empower Your Team.
Headline format examples from the summary: “Top 7 AI-driven Fulfillment Trends Reshaping [Niche] in 2025” / “How Next-Gen Agencies Are Taking Vast Market Share in 2025”.
